{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01A2mmJ8q4qnDP78LfPyj8Gm","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"# Fine-Tuning Vision-Language-Action Models: Optimizing Speed and Success\\\\n\\\\n**Project website: https://openvla-oft.github.io/**\\\\n\\\\n**Paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.19645**\\\\n\\\\n**Summary video: https://youtu.be/T3Zkkr_NTSA**\\\\n\\\\n## System Requirements\\\\n\\\\nInference:\\\\n* 1 GPU with ~16 GB VRAM for LIBERO sim benchmark tasks\\\\n* 1 GPU with ~18 GB VRAM for ALOHA robot tasks\\\\n\\\\nTraining:\\\\n* Between 1-8 GPUs with 27-80 GB, depending on the desired training setup (with default bfloat16 data type). See [this FAQ on our project website](https://openvla-oft.github.io/#train-compute) for details.\\\\n\\\\n## Quick Start\\\\n\\\\nFirst, set up a conda environment (see instructions in [SETUP.md](SETUP.md)).\\\\n\\\\nThen, run the Python script below to download a pretrained OpenVLA-OFT checkpoint and run inference to generate an action chunk:\\\\n\\\\n```python\\\\nimport pickle\\\\nfrom experiments.robot.libero.run_libero_eval import GenerateConfig\\\\nfrom experiments.robot.openvla_utils import get_action_head, get_processor, get_proprio_projector, get_vla, get_vla_action\\\\nfrom prismatic.vla.constants import NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK, PROPRIO_DIM\\\\n\\\\n# Instantiate config (see class GenerateConfig in experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py for definitions)\\\\ncfg = GenerateConfig(\\\\n    pretrained_checkpoint = \\\\\\\"moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial\\\\\\\",\\\\n    use_l1_regression = True,\\\\n    use_diffusion = False,\\\\n    use_film = False,\\\\n    num_images_in_input = 2,\\\\n    use_proprio = True,\\\\n    load_in_8bit = False,\\\\n    load_in_4bit = False,\\\\n    center_crop = True,\\\\n    num_open_loop_steps = NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK,\\\\n    unnorm_key = \\\\\\\"libero_spatial_no_noops\\\\\\\",\\\\n)\\\\n\\\\n# Load OpenVLA-OFT policy and inputs processor\\\\nvla = get_vla(cfg)\\\\nprocessor = get_processor(cfg)\\\\n\\\\n# Load MLP action head to generate continuous actions (via L1 regression)\\\\naction_head = get_action_head(cfg, llm_dim=vla.llm_dim)\\\\n\\\\n# Load proprio projector to map proprio to language embedding space\\\\nproprio_projector = get_proprio_projector(cfg, llm_dim=vla.llm_dim, proprio_dim=PROPRIO_DIM)\\\\n\\\\n# Load sample observation:\\\\n#   observation (dict): {\\\\n#     \\\\\\\"full_image\\\\\\\": primary third-person image,\\\\n#     \\\\\\\"wrist_image\\\\\\\": wrist-mounted camera image,\\\\n#     \\\\\\\"state\\\\\\\": robot proprioceptive state,\\\\n#     \\\\\\\"task_description\\\\\\\": task description,\\\\n#   }\\\\nwith open(\\\\\\\"experiments/robot/libero/sample_libero_spatial_observation.pkl\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"rb\\\\\\\") as file:\\\\n    observation = pickle.load(file)\\\\n\\\\n# Generate robot action chunk (sequence of future actions)\\\\nactions = get_vla_action(cfg, vla, processor, observation, observation[\\\\\\\"task_description\\\\\\\"], action_head, proprio_projector)\\\\nprint(\\\\\\\"Generated action chunk:\\\\\\\")\\\\nfor act in actions:\\\\n    print(act)\\\\n```\\\\n\\\\n## Installation\\\\n\\\\nSee [SETUP.md](SETUP.md) for instructions on setting up the conda environment.\\\\n\\\\n## Training and Evaluation\\\\n\\\\nSee [LIBERO.md](LIBERO.md) for fine-tuning/evaluating on LIBERO simulation benchmark task suites.\\\\n\\\\nSee [ALOHA.md](ALOHA.md) for fine-tuning/evaluating on proprio_dim=PROPRIO_DIM)\\\\n\\\\n# Load sample observation:\\\\n#   observation (dict): {\\\\n#     \\\\\\\"full_image\\\\\\\": primary third-person image,\\\\n#     \\\\\\\"wrist_image\\\\\\\": wrist-mounted camera image,\\\\n#     \\\\\\\"state\\\\\\\": robot proprioceptive state,\\\\n#     \\\\\\\"task_description\\\\\\\": task description,\\\\n#   }\\\\nwith open(\\\\\\\"experiments/robot/libero/sample_libero_spatial_observation.pkl\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"rb\\\\\\\") as file:\\\\n    observation = pickle.load(file)\\\\n\\\\n# Generate robot action chunk action chunk:\\\\\\\")\\\\nfor act in actions:\\\\n    print(act)\\\\n```\\\\n\\\\n## Installation\\\\n\\\\nSee [SETUP.md](SETUP.md) for instructions on setting up the conda environment.\\\\n\\\\n## If you do not receive a response within 2 business days, please email Moo Jin Kim (moojink@cs.stanford.edu) to bring the issue to his attention.\\\\n\\\\n## Citation\\\\n\\\\nIf you use our code in your work, please cite [our paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.19645):\\\\n\\\\n```bibtex\\\\n@article{kim2025fine,\\\\n  title={Fine-Tuning Vision-Language-Action Models: Optimizing Speed and Success},\\\\n  author={Kim, Moo Jin and Finn, Chelsea and Liang, Percy},\\\\n  journal={arXiv preprint arXiv:2502.19645},\\\\n  year={2025}\\\\n}\\\\n```\\\\n\\\", \\\"stderr\\\": \\\"\\\", \\\"return_code\\\": 0}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.31}}\"}","structuredContent":{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"# Fine-Tuning Vision-Language-Action Models: Optimizing Speed and Success\\n\\n**Project website: https://openvla-oft.github.io/**\\n\\n**Paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.19645**\\n\\n**Summary video: https://youtu.be/T3Zkkr_NTSA**\\n\\n## System Requirements\\n\\nInference:\\n* 1 GPU with ~16 GB VRAM for LIBERO sim benchmark tasks\\n* 1 GPU with ~18 GB VRAM for ALOHA robot tasks\\n\\nTraining:\\n* Between 1-8 GPUs with 27-80 GB, depending on the desired training setup (with default bfloat16 data type). See [this FAQ on our project website](https://openvla-oft.github.io/#train-compute) for details.\\n\\n## Quick Start\\n\\nFirst, set up a conda environment (see instructions in [SETUP.md](SETUP.md)).\\n\\nThen, run the Python script below to download a pretrained OpenVLA-OFT checkpoint and run inference to generate an action chunk:\\n\\n```python\\nimport pickle\\nfrom experiments.robot.libero.run_libero_eval import GenerateConfig\\nfrom experiments.robot.openvla_utils import get_action_head, get_processor, get_proprio_projector, get_vla, get_vla_action\\nfrom prismatic.vla.constants import NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK, PROPRIO_DIM\\n\\n# Instantiate config (see class GenerateConfig in experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py for definitions)\\ncfg = GenerateConfig(\\n    pretrained_checkpoint = \\\"moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial\\\",\\n    use_l1_regression = True,\\n    use_diffusion = False,\\n    use_film = False,\\n    num_images_in_input = 2,\\n    use_proprio = True,\\n    load_in_8bit = False,\\n    load_in_4bit = False,\\n    center_crop = True,\\n    num_open_loop_steps = NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK,\\n    unnorm_key = \\\"libero_spatial_no_noops\\\",\\n)\\n\\n# Load OpenVLA-OFT policy and inputs processor\\nvla = get_vla(cfg)\\nprocessor = get_processor(cfg)\\n\\n# Load MLP action head to generate continuous actions (via L1 regression)\\naction_head = get_action_head(cfg, llm_dim=vla.llm_dim)\\n\\n# Load proprio projector to map proprio to language embedding space\\nproprio_projector = get_proprio_projector(cfg, llm_dim=vla.llm_dim, proprio_dim=PROPRIO_DIM)\\n\\n# Load sample observation:\\n#   observation (dict): {\\n#     \\\"full_image\\\": primary third-person image,\\n#     \\\"wrist_image\\\": wrist-mounted camera image,\\n#     \\\"state\\\": robot proprioceptive state,\\n#     \\\"task_description\\\": task description,\\n#   }\\nwith open(\\\"experiments/robot/libero/sample_libero_spatial_observation.pkl\\\", \\\"rb\\\") as file:\\n    observation = pickle.load(file)\\n\\n# Generate robot action chunk (sequence of future actions)\\nactions = get_vla_action(cfg, vla, processor, observation, observation[\\\"task_description\\\"], action_head, proprio_projector)\\nprint(\\\"Generated action chunk:\\\")\\nfor act in actions:\\n    print(act)\\n```\\n\\n## Installation\\n\\nSee [SETUP.md](SETUP.md) for instructions on setting up the conda environment.\\n\\n## Training and Evaluation\\n\\nSee [LIBERO.md](LIBERO.md) for fine-tuning/evaluating on LIBERO simulation benchmark task suites.\\n\\nSee [ALOHA.md](ALOHA.md) for fine-tuning/evaluating on real-world ALOHA robot tasks.\\n\\n## Support\\n\\nIf you run into any issues, please open a new GitHub issue. If you do not receive a response within 2 business days, please email Moo Jin Kim (moojink@cs.stanford.edu) to bring the issue to his attention.\\n\\n## Citation\\n\\nIf you use our code in your work, please cite [our paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.19645):\\n\\n```bibtex\\n@article{kim2025fine,\\n  title={Fine-Tuning Vision-Language-Action Models: Optimizing Speed and Success},\\n  author={Kim, Moo Jin and Finn, Chelsea and Liang, Percy},\\n  journal={arXiv preprint arXiv:2502.19645},\\n  year={2025}\\n}\\n```\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.31}}"}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_019VJsgKWWhVJpR8W9enrpgq","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"# OpenVLA-OFT in the LIBERO Simulation Benchmark\\\\n\\\\n## Relevant Files\\\\n\\\\nEvaluation\\\\n* `experiments/robot/libero/`: LIBERO eval files\\\\n  * `run_libero_eval.py`: LIBERO eval script\\\\n  * `libero_utils.py`: LIBERO eval utils\\\\n* `experiments/robot/`: General eval utils files\\\\n  * `openvla_utils.py`: OpenVLA-specific eval utils\\\\n  * `robot_utils.py`: Other eval utils\\\\n\\\\nTraining\\\\n* `vla-scripts/finetune.py`: VLA fine-tuning script\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n## Setup\\\\n\\\\nSet up a conda environment (see instructions in [SETUP.md](SETUP.md)).\\\\n\\\\nClone and install the [LIBERO repo](https://github.com/Lifelong-Robot-Learning/LIBERO) and required packages:\\\\n\\\\n```bash\\\\ngit clone https://github.com/Lifelong-Robot-Learning/LIBERO.git\\\\npip install -e LIBERO\\\\npip install -r experiments/robot/libero/libero_requirements.txt  # From openvla-oft base dir\\\\n```\\\\n\\\\n(Optional, if you plan to launch training) To download the [LIBERO datasets](https://huggingface.co/datasets/openvla/modified_libero_rlds) that we used in our fine-tuning\\\\nexperiments, run the command below. This will download the LIBERO-Spatial, LIBERO-Object, LIBERO-Goal,\\\\nand LIBERO-10 datasets in RLDS data format (~10 GB total). You can use these to fine-tune OpenVLA or\\\\ntrain other methods. This step is optional since we provide pretrained OpenVLA-OFT checkpoints below.\\\\nNote that these are the same datasets used in the original OpenVLA project. If needed, see details on how to download the original non-RLDS datasets [here](https://github.com/openvla/openvla?tab=readme-ov-file#libero-setup).\\\\n```bash\\\\ngit clone git@hf.co:datasets/openvla/modified_libero_rlds\\\\n```\\\\n\\\\n## Launching LIBERO Evaluations\\\\n\\\\nWe fine-tuned OpenVLA via LoRA (r=32) with our OFT recipe on four LIBERO task suites: LIBERO-Spatial, LIBERO-Object, LIBERO-Goal, and LIBERO-10 (also called LIBERO-Long).\\\\nIn the initial version of our paper, we trained one checkpoint for each LIBERO task suite independently. In an updated version of the paper, we conducted an additional experiment in which we trained a single policy on all four task suites combined (results for this are available in the Additional Experiments section in the Appendix). Overall, the results for the task-specific policies and the combined policy are comparable: 97.1% vs. 96.8% average success rate across the four suites, respectively.\\\\n\\\\nBelow are the four independently trained OpenVLA-OFT checkpoints for LIBERO:\\\\n* [moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial](https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial)\\\\n* [moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-object](https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-object)\\\\n* [moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-goal](https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-goal)\\\\n* [moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-10](https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-10)\\\\n\\\\nBelow is the OpenVLA-OFT checkpoint trained on all four task suites combined:\\\\n* [moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial-object-goal-10](https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial-object-goal-10)\\\\n\\\\nTo start evaluations with one of the independently trained checkpoints, run one of the commands below. Each will automatically download the appropriate checkpoint listed above. You can set the `TRANSFORMERS_CACHE` and `HF_HOME` environment variable to change where the checkpoint files get cached.\\\\n\\\\n```bash\\\\n# Launch LIBERO-Spatial evals\\\\npython experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --pretrained_checkpoint moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --task_suite_name libero_spatial\\\\n\\\\n# Launch LIBERO-Object evals\\\\npython experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --pretrained_checkpoint moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-object \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --task_suite_name libero_object\\\\n\\\\n# Launch LIBERO-Goal evals\\\\npython experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --pretrained_checkpoint moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-goal \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --task_suite_name libero_goal\\\\n\\\\n# Launch LIBERO-10 (LIBERO-Long) evals\\\\npython \\\\n\\\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 960 字符] ...\\\\n\\\\nscript logs results locally. You can also log results in Weights & Biases\\\\n  by setting `--use_wandb True` and specifying `--wandb_project &lt;PROJECT>` and `--wandb_entity &lt;ENTITY>`.\\\\n* The results reported in our paper were obtained using **Python 3.10.14, PyTorch 2.2.0, and our\\\\n  [custom transformers v4.40.1 fork](https://github.com/moojink/transformers-openvla-oft.git)**\\\\n  on an **NVIDIA A100 GPU**, averaged over three random seeds. Please stick to these package versions if possible.\\\\n  Note that results may vary slightly if you use a different GPU than the A100. If the discrepancy is large,\\\\n  please post a GitHub issue, and we will look into it.\\\\n\\\\n## Fine-Tuning on LIBERO Datasets\\\\n\\\\nFirst, download the LIBERO datasets as mentioned above in the Setup section above: `libero_spatial_no_noops`, `libero_object_no_noops`, `libero_goal_no_noops`, `libero_10_no_noops`. (`\\\\\\\"_no_noops\\\\\\\"` stands for no no-op actions, i.e., training samples with near-zero actions are filtered out).\\\\n\\\\nThen, launch the fine-tuning script with the OFT configuration below, replacing `X` in the first line with the number of GPUs. The command below launches fine-tuning on LIBERO-Spatial with the hyperparameters that we used in our paper. Here, batch size 8 per GPU will require ~62 GB VRAM, and batch size 1 per GPU will require ~25 GB VRAM.\\\\n\\\\n```bash\\\\ntorchrun --standalone --nnodes 1 --nproc-per-node X vla-scripts/finetune.py \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --vla_path openvla/openvla-7b \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --data_root_dir /PATH/TO/RLDS/DATASETS/DIR/ \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --dataset_name libero_spatial_no_noops \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --run_root_dir /YOUR/CHECKPOINTS/AND/LOG/DIR/ \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --use_l1_regression True \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --use_diffusion False \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --use_film False \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --num_images_in_input 2 \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --use_proprio True \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --batch_size 8 \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --learning_rate 5e-4 \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --num_steps_before_decay 100000 \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --max_steps 150005 \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --save_freq 10000 \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --save_latest_checkpoint_only False \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --image_aug True \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --lora_rank 32 \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --wandb_entity \\\\\\\"YOUR_WANDB_ENTITY\\\\\\\" \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --wandb_project \\\\\\\"YOUR_WANDB_PROJECT\\\\\\\" \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --run_id_note parallel_dec--8_acts_chunk--continuous_acts--L1_regression--3rd_person_img--wrist_img--proprio_state\\\\n```\\\\n\\\\nThe above training command should reproduce our OpenVLA-OFT results if `X = 8` and the 150K step checkpoint is evaluated.\\\\n\\\\nYou can replace `libero_spatial_no_noops` with `libero_object_no_noops`, `libero_goal_no_noops`, or `libero_10_no_noops`. You can also modify other args — e.g., if you want to train with just one input image from the third-person camera and disable proprio state input, you can set `--num_images_in_input 1` and `--use_proprio False`.\\\\n\\\\nIn general, we recommend fine-tuning until training L1 loss goes below 0.01 and starts to plateau (with the above configuration, it should reach ~0.006 L1 loss on LIBERO-Spatial after 150K gradient steps with 10x LR decay after 100K steps). However, for LIBERO-Goal only, we found that the 50K checkpoint (which was at ~0.02 L1 loss) performed best for unknown reasons. For all other task suites though, we found that the 150K checkpoint performed best.\\\\n\\\\nPlease be sure to test your policy with the same device/GPU used to train it! Otherwise, performance may drop substantially. You may be able to avoid the performance drop if you merge the LoRA weights into the base model on the downstream device used for testing (e.g., if you train on H100 and then merge on A100 before testing on A100). You can see our script [vla-scripts/merge_lora_weights_and_save.py](vla-scripts/merge_lora_weights_and_save.py) for merging the LoRA adapter into the base model offline. It's okay if you already merged LoRA weights into the base OpenVLA model during fine-tuning; you can always redownload the base model and merge again as long as you still have the LoRA adapter (`merge_lora_weights_and_save.py` will handle this for you).\\\\n\\\\nIf you run into any issues, please open a new GitHub issue. If you do not receive a response within 2 business days, please email Moo Jin Kim (moojink@cs.stanford.edu) to bring the issue to his attention.\\\\n\\\", \\\"stderr\\\": \\\"\\\", \\\"return_code\\\": 0}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.31}}\"}"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"20edf378-2c29-4db1-9f30-77d38d22d300","uuid":"7569bf52-faca-476c-a87a-d5aab36f231c","timestamp":"2026-05-22T13:04:25.091Z","tool_use_result":{"content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"# OpenVLA-OFT in the LIBERO Simulation Benchmark\\\\n\\\\n## Relevant Files\\\\n\\\\nEvaluation\\\\n* `experiments/robot/libero/`: LIBERO eval files\\\\n  * `run_libero_eval.py`: LIBERO eval script\\\\n  * `libero_utils.py`: LIBERO eval utils\\\\n* `experiments/robot/`: General eval utils files\\\\n  * `openvla_utils.py`: OpenVLA-specific eval utils\\\\n  * `robot_utils.py`: Other eval utils\\\\n\\\\nTraining\\\\n* `vla-scripts/finetune.py`: VLA fine-tuning script\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n## Setup\\\\n\\\\nSet up a conda environment (see instructions in [SETUP.md](SETUP.md)).\\\\n\\\\nClone and install the [LIBERO repo](https://github.com/Lifelong-Robot-Learning/LIBERO) and required packages:\\\\n\\\\n```bash\\\\ngit clone https://github.com/Lifelong-Robot-Learning/LIBERO.git\\\\npip install -e LIBERO\\\\npip install -r experiments/robot/libero/libero_requirements.txt  # From openvla-oft base dir\\\\n```\\\\n\\\\n(Optional, if you plan to launch training) To download the [LIBERO datasets](https://huggingface.co/datasets/openvla/modified_libero_rlds) that we used in our fine-tuning\\\\nexperiments, run the command below. This will download the LIBERO-Spatial, LIBERO-Object, LIBERO-Goal,\\\\nand LIBERO-10 datasets in RLDS data format (~10 GB total). You can use these to fine-tune OpenVLA or\\\\ntrain other methods. This step is optional since we provide pretrained OpenVLA-OFT checkpoints below.\\\\nNote that these are the same datasets used in the original OpenVLA project. If needed, see details on how to download the original non-RLDS datasets [here](https://github.com/openvla/openvla?tab=readme-ov-file#libero-setup).\\\\n```bash\\\\ngit clone git@hf.co:datasets/openvla/modified_libero_rlds\\\\n```\\\\n\\\\n## Launching LIBERO Evaluations\\\\n\\\\nWe fine-tuned OpenVLA via LoRA (r=32) with our OFT recipe on four LIBERO task suites: LIBERO-Spatial, LIBERO-Object, LIBERO-Goal, and LIBERO-10 (also called LIBERO-Long).\\\\nIn the initial version of our paper, we trained one checkpoint for each LIBERO task suite independently. In an updated version of the paper, we conducted an additional experiment in which we trained a single policy on all four task suites combined (results for this are available in the Additional Experiments section in the Appendix). Overall, the results for the task-specific policies and the combined policy are comparable: 97.1% vs. 96.8% average success rate across the four suites, respectively.\\\\n\\\\nBelow are the four independently trained OpenVLA-OFT checkpoints for LIBERO:\\\\n* [moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial](https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial)\\\\n* [moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-object](https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-object)\\\\n* [moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-goal](https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-goal)\\\\n* [moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-10](https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-10)\\\\n\\\\nBelow is the OpenVLA-OFT checkpoint trained on all four task suites combined:\\\\n* [moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial-object-goal-10](https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial-object-goal-10)\\\\n\\\\nTo start evaluations with one of the independently trained checkpoints, run one of the commands below. Each will automatically download the appropriate checkpoint listed above. You can set the `TRANSFORMERS_CACHE` and `HF_HOME` environment variable to change where the checkpoint files get cached.\\\\n\\\\n```bash\\\\n# Launch LIBERO-Spatial evals\\\\npython experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --pretrained_checkpoint moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --task_suite_name libero_spatial\\\\n\\\\n# Launch LIBERO-Object evals\\\\npython experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --pretrained_checkpoint moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-object \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --task_suite_name libero_object\\\\n\\\\n# Launch LIBERO-Goal evals\\\\npython experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --pretrained_checkpoint moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-goal \\\\\\\\\\\\n  --task_suite_name libero_goal\\\\n\\\\n# Launch LIBERO-10 (LIBERO-Long) evals\\\\npython \\\\n\\\\n... Overall, the results for the task-specific policies and the combined policy are comparable: 97.1% vs. 96.8% average success rate across the four suites, respectively.\\n\\nBelow are the four independently trained OpenVLA-OFT checkpoints for LIBERO:\\n* [moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial](https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial)\\n* [moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-object](https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-object)\\n* [moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-goal](https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-goal)\\n* [moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-10](https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-10)\\n\\nBelow is the OpenVLA-OFT checkpoint trained on all four task suites combined:\\n* [moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial-object-goal-10](https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial-object-goal-10)\\n\\nTo start evaluations with one of the independently trained checkpoints, run one of the commands below. Here, batch size 8 per GPU will require ~62 GB VRAM, and batch size 1 per GPU will require ~25 GB VRAM.\\n\\n```bash\\ntorchrun --standalone --nnodes 1 --nproc-per-node X vla-scripts/finetune.py \\\\\\n  --vla_path openvla/openvla-7b \\\\\\n  --data_root_dir /PATH/TO/RLDS/DATASETS/DIR/ \\\\\\n  --dataset_name libero_spatial_no_noops \\\\\\n  --run_root_dir /YOUR/CHECKPOINTS/AND/LOG/DIR/ \\\\\\n  --use_l1_regression True \\\\\\n  --use_diffusion False \\\\\\n  --use_film False \\\\\\n  --num_images_in_input 2 \\\\\\n  --use_proprio True \\\\\\n  --batch_size 8 \\\\\\n  --learning_rate 5e-4 \\\\\\n  --num_steps_before_decay 100000 \\\\\\n  --max_steps 150005 \\\\\\n  --save_freq 10000 \\\\\\n  --save_latest_checkpoint_only False \\\\\\n  --image_aug True \\\\\\n  --lora_rank 32 \\\\\\n  --wandb_entity \\\"YOUR_WANDB_ENTITY\\\" \\\\\\n  --wandb_project \\\"YOUR_WANDB_PROJECT\\\" \\\\\\n  --run_id_note parallel_dec--8_acts_chunk--continuous_acts--L1_regression--3rd_person_img--wrist_img--proprio_state\\n```\\n\\nThe above training command should reproduce our OpenVLA-OFT results if `X = 8` and the 150K step checkpoint is evaluated.\\n\\nYou can replace `libero_spatial_no_noops` with `libero_object_no_noops`, `libero_goal_no_noops`, or `libero_10_no_noops`. You can also modify other args — e.g., if you want to train with just one input image from the third-person camera and disable proprio state input, you can set `--num_images_in_input 1` and `--use_proprio False`.\\n\\nIn general, we recommend fine-tuning until training L1 loss goes below 0.01 and starts to plateau (with the above configuration, it should reach ~0.006 L1 loss on LIBERO-Spatial after 150K gradient steps with 10x LR decay after 100K steps). get_image_resize_size,\\n    get_model,\\n    invert_gripper_action,\\n    normalize_gripper_action,\\n    set_seed_everywhere,\\n)\\nfrom prismatic.vla.constants import NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK\\n\\n\\n# Define task suite constants\\nclass TaskSuite(str, Enum):\\n    LIBERO_SPATIAL = \\\"libero_spatial\\\"\\n    LIBERO_OBJECT = \\\"libero_object\\\"\\n    LIBERO_GOAL = \\\"libero_goal\\\"\\n    LIBERO_10 = \\\"libero_10\\\"\\n    LIBERO_90 = \\\"libero_90\\\"\\n\\n\\n# Define max steps for each task suite\\nTASK_MAX_STEPS = {\\n    TaskSuite.LIBERO_SPATIAL: 220,  # longest training demo has 193 steps\\n    TaskSuite.LIBERO_OBJECT: 280,  # longest training demo has 254 steps\\n    TaskSuite.LIBERO_GOAL: 300,  # longest training demo has 270 steps\\n    TaskSuite.LIBERO_10: 520,  # longest training demo has 505 steps\\n    TaskSuite.LIBERO_90: 400,  # longest training demo has 373 steps\\n}\\n\\n\\n# Set up logging\\nlogging.basicConfig(\\n    level=logging.INFO,\\n    format=\\\"%(asctime)s [%(levelname)s] %(message)s\\\",\\n    handlers=[logging.StreamHandler()],\\n)\\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\\n\\n\\n@dataclass\\nclass GenerateConfig:\\n    # fmt: off\\n\\n    #################################################################################################################\\n    # Model-specific parameters\\n    #################################################################################################################\\n    model_family: str = \\\"openvla\\\"                    # Model family\\n    pretrained_checkpoint: Union[str, Path] = \\\"\\\"     # Pretrained checkpoint path\\n\\n    use_l1_regression: bool = True                   # If True, uses continuous action head with L1 regression objective\\n    use_diffusion: bool = False                      # If True, uses continuous action head with diffusion modeling objective (DDIM)\\n    num_diffusion_steps_train: int = 50              # (When `diffusion==True`) Number of diffusion steps used for training\\n    num_diffusion_steps_inference: int = 50          # (When `diffusion==True`) Number of diffusion steps used for inference\\n    use_film: bool = False                           # If True, uses FiLM to infuse language inputs into visual features\\n    num_images_in_input: int = 2                     # Number of images in the VLA input (default: 1)\\n    use_proprio: bool = True                         # Whether to include proprio state in input\\n\\n    center_crop: bool = True                         # Center crop? (if trained w/ random crop image aug)\\n    num_open_loop_steps: int = 8                     # Number of actions to execute open-loop before requerying policy\\n\\n    lora_rank: int = 32                              # Rank of LoRA weight matrix (MAKE SURE THIS MATCHES TRAINING!)\\n\\n    unnorm_key: Union[str, Path] = \\\"\\\"                # Action un-normalization key\\n\\n    load_in_8bit: bool = False                       # (For OpenVLA only) Load with 8-bit quantization\\n    load_in_4bit: bool = False                       # (For OpenVLA only) Load with 4-bit quantization\\n\\n    #################################################################################################################\\n    # LIBERO environment-specific parameters\\n    #################################################################################################################\\n    task_suite_name: str = TaskSuite.LIBERO_SPATIAL  # Task suite\\n    num_steps_wait: int = 10                         # Number of steps to wait for objects to stabilize in sim\\n    num_trials_per_task: int = 50                    # Number of rollouts per task\\n    initial_states_path: str = \\\"DEFAULT\\\"             # \\\"DEFAULT\\\", or path to initial states JSON file\\n    env_img_res: int = 256                           # Resolution for environment images (not policy input resolution)\\n\\n    #################################################################################################################\\n    # Utils\\n    #################################################################################################################\\n    run_id_note: Optional[str] = None                # Extra note to add to end of run ID for logging\\n    local_log_dir: str = \\\"./experiments/logs\\\"        # Local directory for eval logs\\n\\n    use_wandb: bool = False                          # Whether to also log results in Weights & Biases\\n    wandb_entity: str = \\\"your-wandb-entity\\\"          # Name of WandB entity\\n    wandb_project: str = \\\"your-wandb-project\\\"        # Name of WandB project\\n\\n    seed: int = 7                                    # Random Seed (for reproducibility)\\n\\n    # fmt: on\\n\\n\\ndef validate_config(cfg: GenerateConfig) -> None:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Validate configuration parameters.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    assert cfg.pretrained_checkpoint is not None, \\\"pretrained_checkpoint must not be None!\\\"\\n\\n    if \\\"image_aug\\\" in str(cfg.pretrained_checkpoint):\\n        assert cfg.center_crop, \\\"Expecting `center_crop==True` because model was trained with image augmentations!\\\"\\n\\n    assert not (cfg.load_in_8bit and cfg.load_in_4bit), \\\"Cannot use both 8-bit and 4-bit quantization!\\\"\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.32}}"}}}

off\\n\\n    #################################################################################################################\\n    # Model-specific parameters\\n # (When `diffusion==True`) Number of diffusion steps used for training\\n    num_diffusion_steps_inference: int = 50          # (When `diffusion==True`) Number of diffusion steps used for inference\\n (if trained w/ random crop image aug)\\n    num_open_loop_steps: int = 8                     # Number of actions to execute open-loop before requerying policy\\n\\n    lora_rank: int = 32                              # Rank of LoRA weight matrix (MAKE SURE THIS MATCHES TRAINING!)\\n\\n    unnorm_key: Union[str, Path] = \\\"\\\"                # Action un-normalization key\\n\\n    load_in_8bit: bool = False                       # (For OpenVLA only) Load with 8-bit quantization\\n    load_in_4bit: bool = False                       # (For OpenVLA only) Load with 4-bit quantization\\n\\n    ##############################################################################################################\\n\\n... OpenVLA-OFT significantly improves upon the base OpenVLA model by incorporating optimized fine-tuning techniques.\\\\n\\\\nProject Page: https://openvla-oft.github.io/\\\\n\\\\nCode: https://github.com/openvla-oft/openvla-oft\\\\n\\\\nSee here for other OpenVLA-OFT checkpoints: https://huggingface.co/moojink?search_models=oft\\\\n## Quick Start\\\\nThis example demonstrates generating an action chunk using a pretrained OpenVLA-OFT checkpoint.  Ensure you have set up the conda environment as described in the GitHub README.\\\\n```python\\\\nimport pickle\\\\nfrom experiments.robot.libero.run_libero_eval import GenerateConfig\\\\nfrom experiments.robot.openvla_utils import get_action_head, get_processor, get_proprio_projector, get_vla, get_vla_action\\\\nfrom prismatic.vla.constants import NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK, PROPRIO_DIM\\\\n# Instantiate config (see class GenerateConfig in experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py for definitions)\\\\ncfg = GenerateConfig(\\\\n    pretrained_checkpoint = \\\\\\\"moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial\\\\\\\",\\\\n    use_l1_regression = True,\\\\n    use_diffusion = False,\\\\n    use_film = False,\\\\n    num_images_in_input = 2,\\\\n    use_proprio = True,\\\\n    load_in_8bit = False,\\\\n    load_in_4bit = False,\\\\n    center_crop = True,\\\\n    num_open_loop_steps = NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK,\\\\n    unnorm_key = \\\\\\\"libero_spatial_no_noops\\\\\\\",\\\\n)\\\\n# Load OpenVLA-OFT policy and inputs processor\\\\nvla = get_vla(cfg)\\\\nprocessor = get_processor(cfg)\\\\n\\\\n# Load MLP action head to generate continuous actions (via L1 regression)\\\\naction_head = get_action_head(cfg, llm_dim=vla.llm_dim)\\\\n# Load proprio projector to map proprio to language embedding space\\\\nproprio_projector = get_proprio_projector(cfg, llm_dim=vla.llm_dim, proprio_dim=PROPRIO_DIM)\\\\n\\\\n# Load sample observation:\\\\n#   observation (dict): {\\\\n#     \\\\\\\"full_image\\\\\\\": primary third-person image,\\\\n#     \\\\\\\"wrist_image\\\\\\\": wrist-mounted camera image,\\\\n#     \\\\\\\"state\\\\\\\": robot proprioceptive state,\\\\n#     \\\\\\\"task_description\\\\\\\": task description,\\\\n#   }\\\\nwith open(\\\\\\\"experiments/robot/libero/sample_libero_spatial_observation.pkl\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"rb\\\\\\\") as file:\\\\n    observation = pickle.load(file)\\\\n# Generate robot action chunk (sequence of future actions)\\\\nactions = get_vla_action(cfg, vla, processor, observation, observation[\\\\\\\"task_description\\\\\\\"], action_head, proprio_projector)\\\\nprint(\\\\\\\"Generated action chunk:\\\\\\\")\\\\nfor act in actions:\\\\n    print(act)\\\\n```\\\\n## Citation\\\\n```bibtex\\\\n@article{kim2025fine,\\\\n  title={Fine-Tuning Vision-Language-Action Models: Optimizing Speed and Success},\\\\n  author={Kim, Moo Jin and Finn, Chelsea and Liang, Percy},\\\\n  journal={arXiv preprint arXiv:2502.19645},\\\\n Ensure you have set up the conda environment as described in the GitHub README.\\n```python\\nimport pickle\\nfrom experiments.robot.libero.run_libero_eval import GenerateConfig\\nfrom experiments.robot.openvla_utils import get_action_head, get_processor, get_proprio_projector, get_vla, get_vla_action\\nfrom prismatic.vla.constants import NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK, PROPRIO_DIM\\n# True,\\n    load_in_8bit = False,\\n    load_in_4bit = False,\\n    center_crop = True,\\n    num_open_loop_steps = NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK,\\n    unnorm_key = \\\"libero_spatial_no_noops\\\",\\n)\\n# Load OpenVLA-OFT policy and inputs processor\\nvla = get_vla(cfg)\\nprocessor = get_processor(cfg)\\n\\n# Load MLP action head to generate continuous actions (via L1 regression)\\naction_head = get_action_head(cfg, llm_dim=vla.llm_dim)\\n# Load proprio projector to map proprio to language embedding space\\nproprio_projector = get_proprio_projector(cfg, llm_dim=vla.llm_dim, proprio_dim=PROPRIO_DIM)\\n\\n# Load sample observation:\\n#   observation (dict): {\\n#     \\\"full_image\\\": primary third-person image,\\n#     \\\"wrist_image\\\": wrist-mounted camera image,\\n#     \\\"state\\\": robot proprioceptive state,\\n#     \\\"task_description\\\": task description,\\n#   }\\nwith {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01Swor3AQ3PdnFS3rbUzNGvD","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"mujoco 3.8.1 with robosuite 1.4.1. Let me check if there's a known issue with mujoco 3.x and LIBERO/robosuite. \n\nThe OFT paper was likely developed with mujoco 2.x (mujoco bindings, post mujoco-py era). Let me check if there are specific mujoco version constraints.\n\nActually, robosuite 1.4.1 requires `mujoco` (new bindings), and it should work with mujoco 2.x and 3.x. \n\nEven if there are rendering or physics differences between mujoco versions, a drop from 97% to 0% success rate seems too severe to be just a version mismatch. That's a fundamental failure, not a subtle behavioral change. So I should look at other culprits: the checkpoint might be corrupted or improperly downloaded, the action normalization could be misconfigured, or there's a bug in the evaluation code itself.\n\nThe most direct approach would be to check the dataset statistics file to understand what normalization parameters are being applied, then trace through the action generation and execution pipeline to see where things break down. Rather than modifying the official code, I could examine the rollout videos to observe what's actually happening in the simulation. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01Asa4bNv3Up3KrUnQBCwqA6","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_019AYmGojsxQqDnkVyax36g3","name":"mcp__agent-ml-research__ssh_execute","input":{"server":"bjb2-40593","command":"cat > /tmp/debug_action.py << 'PYEOF'\nimport sys\nsys.path.insert(0, \"/root/autodl-tmp/openvla-oft-official\")\nimport os\nos.environ[\"MUJOCO_GL\"] = \"egl\"\nimport torch\nimport numpy as np\nfrom experiments.robot.libero.run_libero_eval import GenerateConfig\nfrom experiments.robot.openvla_utils import get_action_head, get_processor, get_proprio_projector, get_vla, get_vla_action\nfrom experiments.robot.libero.libero_utils import get_libero_env, get_libero_image, get_libero_wrist_image\nfrom libero.libero import benchmark\nfrom prismatic.vla.constants import NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK, PROPRIO_DIM\n\ncfg = GenerateConfig(\n    pretrained_checkpoint=\"/root/autodl-tmp/models/openvla-oft/object/\",\n    task_suite_name=\"libero_object\",\n    use_l1_regression=True,\n    use_diffusion=False,\n    use_film=False,\n    num_images_in_input=2,\n    use_proprio=True,\n    center_crop=True,\n    num_open_loop_steps=NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK,\n)\n\n# Load model\nvla = get_vla(cfg)\nprocessor = get_processor(cfg)\naction_head = get_action_head(cfg, llm_dim=vla.llm_dim)\nproprio_projector = get_proprio_projector(cfg, llm_dim=vla.llm_dim, proprio_dim=PROPRIO_DIM)\n\n# Check unnorm key\nfrom experiments.robot.libero.run_libero_eval import check_unnorm_key\ncheck_unnorm_key(cfg, vla)\nprint(f\"unnorm_key: {cfg.unnorm_key}\")\nprint(f\"norm_stats keys: {list(vla.norm_stats.keys())}\")\nprint(f\"norm_stats for {cfg.unnorm_key}: {vla.norm_stats[cfg.unnorm_key]}\")\n\n# Create env and get observation\nbenchmark_dict = benchmark.get_benchmark_dict()\ntask_suite = benchmark_dict[\"libero_object\"]()\ntask = task_suite.get_task(0)\nenv, task_desc = get_libero_env(task, \"openvla\", resolution=256)\nprint(f\"Task: {task_desc}\")\n\nenv.reset()\ninit_states = task_suite.get_task_init_states(0)\nobs = env.set_init_state(init_states[0])\n\n# Get observation\nimg = get_libero_image(obs)\nwrist_img = get_libero_wrist_image(obs)\nprint(f\"Image shape: {np.array(img).shape}, Wrist image shape: {np.array(wrist_img).shape}\")\nprint(f\"Proprio state: {obs['robot0_eef_pos']}, {obs['robot0_eef_quat']}, {obs['robot0_gripper_qpos']}\")\n\n# Build observation dict\nobservation = {\n    \"full_image\": img,\n    \"wrist_image\": wrist_img,\n    \"state\": np.concatenate([obs[\"robot0_eef_pos\"], obs[\"robot0_eef_quat\"], obs[\"robot0_gripper_qpos\"]]),\n    \"task_description\": task_desc,\n}\n\n# Generate actions\nactions = get_vla_action(cfg, vla, processor, observation, task_desc, action_head, proprio_projector)\nprint(f\"\\nGenerated action chunk ({len(actions)} actions):\")\nfor i, act in enumerate(actions):\n    print(f\"  Action {i}: {act}\")\nprint(f\"\\nAction stats: min={np.min(actions):.4f}, max={np.max(actions):.4f}, mean={np.mean(actions):.4f}\")\nPYEOF\necho \"Script written\"","account":"root"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2414,"cache_read_input_tokens":83184,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2414},"output_tokens":8,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"20edf378-2c29-4db1-9f30-77d38d22d300","uuid":"d9d0a90e-5818-4ae7-8b4c-dff1b903a259"}

import NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n# Define task suite constants\\\\nclass TaskSuite(str, Enum):\\\\n    LIBERO_SPATIAL = \\\\\\\"libero_spatial\\\\\\\"\\\\n    LIBERO_OBJECT = \\\\\\\"libero_object\\\\\\\"\\\\n    LIBERO_GOAL = \\\\\\\"libero_goal\\\\\\\"\\\\n    LIBERO_10 = \\\\\\\"libero_10\\\\\\\"\\\\n    LIBERO_90 = \\\\\\\"libero_90\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n# Define max family\\\\n    pretrained_checkpoint: Union[str, Path] = \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"     # Pretrained checkpoint path\\\\n\\\\n    use_l1_regression: bool = True                   # If True, uses continuous Define task suite constants\\nclass TaskSuite(str, Enum):\\n    LIBERO_SPATIAL = \\\"libero_spatial\\\"\\n    LIBERO_OBJECT = \\\"libero_object\\\"\\n    LIBERO_GOAL = \\\"libero_goal\\\"\\n    LIBERO_10 = \\\"libero_10\\\"\\n    LIBERO_90 = \\\"libero_90\\\"\\n\\n\\n# Define max steps for each task suite\\nTASK_MAX_STEPS = {\\n    TaskSuite.LIBERO_SPATIAL: 220,  # longest training demo has 193 steps\\n    TaskSuite.LIBERO_OBJECT: 280,  # longest training demo has 254 steps\\n    TaskSuite.LIBERO_GOAL: 300,  # longest training demo has 270 steps\\n    TaskSuite.LIBERO_10: 520,  # longest training demo has 505 steps\\n    TaskSuite.LIBERO_90: 400,  # longest training demo has 373 steps\\n}\\n\\n\\n# Set up logging\\nlogging.basicConfig(\\n    level=logging.INFO,\\n    format=\\\"%(asctime)s [%(levelname)s] %(message)s\\\",\\n    handlers=[logging.StreamHandler()],\\n)\\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\\n\\n\\n@dataclass\\nclass GenerateConfig:\\n    # fmt: off\\n\\n    #################################################################################################################\\n    # Model-specific parameters\\n    #################################################################################################################\\n    model_family: str = \\\"openvla\\\"                    # Model family\\n objective\\n    use_diffusion: bool = False                      # If True, uses continuous action head with diffusion modeling objective (DDIM)\\n    num_diffusion_steps_train: int = 50              # (When `diffusion==True`) Number of diffusion steps used for training\\n    num_diffusion_steps_inference: int = 50          # (When `diffusion==True`) Number of diffusion steps used for inference\\n    use_film: bool = False                           # If True, uses FiLM to infuse language inputs into visual features\\n    num_images_in_input: int = 2                     # Number of images in the VLA input (default: 1)\\n    use_proprio: bool = True                         # Whether to include proprio state in input\\n\\n    center_crop: bool = True                         # Center crop? [截断: 输出过长，省略 3013 字符] ...\\n\\nction_head, proprio_projector, noisy_action_projector, processor\\n\\n\\ndef check_unnorm_key(cfg: GenerateConfig, model) -> None:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Check that the model contains the action un-normalization key.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Initialize unnorm_key\\n    unnorm_key = cfg.task_suite_name\\n\\n    # In some cases, the key must be manually modified (e.g. after training on a modified version of the dataset\\n    # with the suffix \\\"_no_noops\\\" in the dataset name)\\n    if unnorm_key not in model.norm_stats and f\\\"{unnorm_key}_no_noops\\\" in model.norm_stats:\\n        unnorm_key = f\\\"{unnorm_key}_no_noops\\\"\\n\\n    assert unnorm_key in model.norm_stats, f\\\"Action un-norm key {unnorm_key} not found in VLA `norm_stats`!\\\"\\n\\n    # Set the unnorm_key in cfg\\n    cfg.unnorm_key = unnorm_key\\n\\n\\ndef setup_logging(cfg: GenerateConfig):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Set up logging to file and optionally to wandb.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Create run ID\\n    run_id = f\\\"EVAL-{cfg.task_suite_name}-{cfg.model_family}-{DATE_TIME}\\\"\\n    if cfg.run_id_note is not None:\\n        run_id += f\\\"--{cfg.run_id_note}\\\"\\n\\n    # Set up local logging\\n    os.makedirs(cfg.local_log_dir, exist_ok=True)\\n    local_log_filepath = os.path.join(cfg.local_log_dir, run_id + \\\".txt\\\")\\n    log_file = open(local_log_filepath, \\\"w\\\")\\n    logger.info(f\\\"Logging to local log file: {local_log_filepath}\\\")\\n\\n    # Initialize Weights & Biases logging if enabled\\n    if cfg.use_wandb:\\n        wandb.init(\\n            entity=cfg.wandb_entity,\\n            project=cfg.wandb_project,\\n            name=run_id,\\n        )\\n\\n    return log_file, local_log_filepath, run_id\\n\\n\\ndef log_message(message: str, log_file=None):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Log a message to console and optionally to a log file.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    logger.info(message)\\n    if log_file:\\n        log_file.write(message + \\\"\\\\n\\\")\\n        log_file.flush()\\n\\n\\ndef load_initial_states(cfg: GenerateConfig, task_suite, task_id: int, log_file=None):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Load initial states for the given task.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Get default initial states\\n    initial_states = task_suite.get_task_init_states(task_id)\\n\\n    # If using custom initial states, load them from file\\n    if cfg.initial_states_path != \\\"DEFAULT\\\":\\n        with open(cfg.initial_states_path, \\\"r\\\") as f:\\n            all_initial_states = json.load(f)\\n        log_message(f\\\"Using initial states from {cfg.initial_states_path}\\\", log_file)\\n        return initial_states, all_initial_states\\n    else:\\n        log_message(\\\"Using default initial states\\\", log_file)\\n        return initial_states, None\\n\\n\\ndef prepare_observation(obs, resize_size):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Prepare observation for policy input.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Get preprocessed images\\n    img = get_libero_image(obs)\\n    wrist_img = get_libero_wrist_image(obs)\\n\\n    # Resize images to size expected by model\\n    img_resized = resize_image_for_policy(img, resize_size)\\n    wrist_img_resized = resize_image_for_policy(wrist_img, resize_size)\\n\\n    # Prepare observations dict\\n    observation = {\\n        \\\"full_image\\\": img_resized,\\n        \\\"wrist_image\\\": wrist_img_resized,\\n        \\\"state\\\": np.concatenate(\\n            (obs[\\\"robot0_eef_pos\\\"], quat2axisangle(obs[\\\"robot0_eef_quat\\\"]), obs[\\\"robot0_gripper_qpos\\\"])\\n        ),\\n    }\\n\\n    return observation, img  # Return both processed observation and original image for replay\\n\\n\\ndef process_action(action, model_family):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Process action before sending to environment.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Normalize gripper action [0,1] -> [-1,+1] because the environment expects the latter\\n    action = normalize_gripper_action(action, binarize=True)\\n\\n    # [OpenVLA] The dataloader flips the sign of the gripper action to align with other datasets\\n    # (0 = close, 1 = open), so flip it back (-1 = open, +1 = close) before executing the action\\n    if model_family == \\\"openvla\\\":\\n        action = invert_gripper_action(action)\\n\\n    return action\\n\\n\\ndef run_episode(\\n    cfg: GenerateConfig,\\n    env,\\n    task_description: str,\\n    model,\\n    resize_size,\\n    processor=None,\\n    action_head=None,\\n    proprio_projector=None,\\n    noisy_action_projector=None,\\n    initial_state=None,\\n    log_file=None,\\n):\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.32}}"}}}

get_image_resize_size(cfg)\nprint(f\"unnorm_key: {cfg.unnorm_key}\")\nprint(f\"resize_size: {resize_size}\")\n\n# Create env\nbenchmark_dict = benchmark.get_benchmark_dict()\ntask_suite = benchmark_dict[\"libero_object\"]()\ntask = task_suite.get_task(0)\nenv, task_desc = get_libero_env(task, \"openvla\", resolution=256)\nprint(f\"Task: {task_desc}\")\n\n# Reset and action = process_action(actions[step], \"openvla\")\n    obs, reward, done, info = env.step(action.tolist())\n    print(f\"Step {step}: action={action}, done={done}\")\nPYEOF\necho \"Script {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01SKf8RHoQAa8bcviVBVTnR3","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Now I can see the action values generated by the model. Let me analyze them:\n\nGenerated actions (before processing):\n```\nAction 0: [0.193 0.197 0.070 0.002 -0.000 -0.002 0.123]\nAction 1: [0.251 0.328 0.098 0.001 -0.002 -0.005 0.256]\n...\n```\n\nAfter processing (normalize_gripper_action + invert_gripper_action):\n```\nStep 0: action=[0.193 0.197 0.070 0.002 -0.000 -0.002 1.000], done=False\n\n\nStep 1: action=[0.251 0.328 0.098 0.001 -0.002 -0.005 1.000], done=False\n```\n\nThe gripper action is being binarized and inverted: all pre-processing gripper values fall in the [0.123, 0.256] range, which after normalization and binarization map to -1, then inversion flips them to +1. So the gripper stays locked at +1 (closed) throughout. Let me double-check this gripper processing logic to make sure I understand it correctly.\n\nThe normalization function maps [0,1] to [-1,+1] with a 0.5 threshold, so values below 0.5 become -1 and those at or above become +1. Then the inversion function negates the sign. Since all the gripper values are well below 0.5, they normalize to -1 and then invert to +1, which represents a closed gripper in the LIBERO convention. The arm movements show positive x and y displacement with minimal z change, suggesting forward and rightward motion.\n\nThe model is generating structured, reasonable-magnitude actions rather than noise, which confirms the weights loaded correctly. However, for a pick-and-place task like retrieving the alphabet soup and placing it in a basket, the gripper should start open to approach the object, then close to grasp it—but the model is predicting a closed gripper from the beginning. This suggests either a timing issue where the model expects the gripper to already be open, or a fundamental misunderstanding of the task sequence.\n\nLooking at the LIBERO action conventions more carefully, I need to trace through the gripper transformation pipeline: the raw model outputs around 0.1-0.4 get normalized to -1 (since they're below 0.5), then inverted to +1, which maps to \"close\" in the environment convention. So every predicted action is commanding the gripper to close, which is clearly wrong for initiating a pick-and-place task.\n\nNow I'm wondering about the initial gripper state from the proprioceptive input—the last two values in the observation vector should tell me whether the gripper starts open or closed, which would help explain whether this constant \"close\" command is actually a problem or just the natural starting state. then down). The robot's reaching toward something with minimal orientation changes, which makes sense for LIBERO tasks where the gripper stays mostly level. The gripper values being under 0.5 suggests it's approaching with a closed gripper, which could be correct if it opens later. Let me reconsider the action processing—these are 7D delta actions (dx, dy, dz, droll, dpitch, dyaw, gripper) that execute in simulation, and the magnitudes look reasonable for small incremental movements.\n\nNow I'm checking the unnormalization against the norm_stats to verify the action values are being scaled correctly...\n\nThe gripper dimension is masked as False, meaning it's not normalized, so those raw model outputs of 0.12-0.43 get binarized to -1 (close) for the first 8 actions. The actions themselves look structured and intentional, so the consistent 100% eval failure must stem from something else—maybe the action magnitudes or how they're being applied to the environment.\n\nI'm considering whether the delta actions (0.2-0.5 for translation) fall within LIBERO's expected range, or if there's a mismatch between the action space the model outputs and what the environment actually uses. There could also be rendering differences with mujoco 3.8.1 that cause the visual policy to produce incorrect actions. Let me check how the LIBERO environment configures its action space.\n\nI think the most productive path forward is to investigate the mujoco version compatibility with robosuite 1.4.1, since that's likely the root cause of the zero success rate. I could also verify whether the model weights were properly merged by comparing them against the base OpenVLA model, or test with the HuggingFace version directly to rule out local checkpoint {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01J18WvTAVJP5xFyrjUbdtCm","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Utils for evaluating policies in LIBERO simulation environments.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\nimport math\\\\nimport os\\\\n\\\\nimport imageio\\\\nimport numpy as np\\\\nimport tensorflow as tf\\\\nfrom libero.libero import get_libero_path\\\\nfrom libero.libero.envs import OffScreenRenderEnv\\\\n\\\\nfrom experiments.robot.robot_utils import (\\\\n    DATE,\\\\n    DATE_TIME,\\\\n)\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef get_libero_env(task, model_family, resolution=256):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Initializes and returns the LIBERO environment, along with the task description.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    task_description = task.language\\\\n    task_bddl_file = os.path.join(get_libero_path(\\\\\\\"bddl_files\\\\\\\"), task.problem_folder, task.bddl_file)\\\\n    env_args = {\\\\\\\"bddl_file_name\\\\\\\": task_bddl_file, \\\\\\\"camera_heights\\\\\\\": resolution, \\\\\\\"camera_widths\\\\\\\": resolution}\\\\n    env = OffScreenRenderEnv(**env_args)\\\\n    env.seed(0)  # IMPORTANT: seed seems to affect object positions even when using fixed initial state\\\\n    return env, task_description\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef get_libero_dummy_action(model_family: str):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Get dummy/no-op action, used to roll out the simulation while the robot does nothing.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    return [0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, -1]\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef get_libero_image(obs):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Extracts third-person image from observations and preprocesses it.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    img = obs[\\\\\\\"agentview_image\\\\\\\"]\\\\n    img = img[::-1, ::-1]  # IMPORTANT: rotate 180 degrees to match train preprocessing\\\\n    return img\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef get_libero_wrist_image(obs):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Extracts wrist camera image from observations and preprocesses it.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    img = obs[\\\\\\\"robot0_eye_in_hand_image\\\\\\\"]\\\\n    img = img[::-1, ::-1]  # IMPORTANT: rotate 180 degrees to match train preprocessing\\\\n    return img\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef save_rollout_video(rollout_images, idx, success, task_description, log_file=None):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Saves an MP4 replay of an episode.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    rollout_dir = f\\\\\\\"./rollouts/{DATE}\\\\\\\"\\\\n    os.makedirs(rollout_dir, exist_ok=True)\\\\n    processed_task_description = task_description.lower().replace(\\\\\\\" \\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"_\\\\\\\").replace(\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"_\\\\\\\").replace(\\\\\\\".\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"_\\\\\\\")[:50]\\\\n    mp4_path = f\\\\\\\"{rollout_dir}/{DATE_TIME}--openvla_oft--episode={idx}--success={success}--task={processed_task_description}.mp4\\\\\\\"\\\\n    video_writer = imageio.get_writer(mp4_path, fps=30)\\\\n    for img in rollout_images:\\\\n        video_writer.append_data(img)\\\\n    video_writer.close()\\\\n    print(f\\\\\\\"Saved rollout MP4 at path {mp4_path}\\\\\\\")\\\\n    if log_file is not None:\\\\n        log_file.write(f\\\\\\\"Saved rollout MP4 at path {mp4_path}\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\")\\\\n    return mp4_path\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef quat2axisangle(quat):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    Copied from robosuite: https://github.com/ARISE-Initiative/robosuite/blob/eafb81f54ffc104f905ee48a16bb15f059176ad3/robosuite/utils/transform_utils.py#L490C1-L512C55\\\\n\\\\n    Converts quaternion to axis-angle format.\\\\n    Returns a unit vector direction scaled by its angle in radians.\\\\n\\\\n    Args:\\\\n        quat (np.array): (x,y,z,w) vec4 float angles\\\\n\\\\n    Returns:\\\\n        np.array: (ax,ay,az) axis-angle exponential coordinates\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    # clip quaternion\\\\n    if quat[3] > 1.0:\\\\n        quat[3] = 1.0\\\\n    elif quat[3] < -1.0:\\\\n        quat[3] = -1.0\\\\n\\\\n    den = np.sqrt(1.0 - quat[3] * quat[3])\\\\n    if math.isclose(den, 0.0):\\\\n        # IMPORTANT: rotate 180 degrees to match train preprocessing\\n    return img\\n\\n\\ndef save_rollout_video(rollout_images, idx, success, task_description, log_file=None):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Saves an MP4 replay of an episode.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    rollout_dir = f\\\"./rollouts/{DATE}\\\"\\n    os.makedirs(rollout_dir, exist_ok=True)\\n    processed_task_description = task_description.lower().replace(\\\" \\\", \\\"_\\\").replace(\\\"\\\\n\\\", \\\"_\\\").replace(\\\".\\\", \\\"_\\\")[:50]\\n    mp4_path = f\\\"{rollout_dir}/{DATE_TIME}--openvla_oft--episode={idx}--success={success}--task={processed_task_description}.mp4\\\"\\n    video_writer = imageio.get_writer(mp4_path, fps=30)\\n    for img in rollout_images:\\n        video_writer.append_data(img)\\n    video_writer.close()\\n    print(f\\\"Saved rollout MP4 at path {mp4_path}\\\")\\n    if log_file is not None:\\n        log_file.write(f\\\"Saved rollout MP4 at path {mp4_path}\\\\n\\\")\\n    return mp4_path\\n\\n\\ndef quat2axisangle(quat):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Copied from robosuite: https://github.com/ARISE-Initiative/robosuite/blob/eafb81f54ffc104f905ee48a16bb15f059176ad3/robosuite/utils/transform_utils.py#L490C1-L512C55\\n\\n    Converts quaternion to axis-angle format.\\n    Returns a unit vector direction scaled by its angle in radians.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        quat (np.array): (x,y,z,w) vec4 float angles\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        np.array: (ax,ay,az) axis-angle exponential coordinates\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # clip quaternion\\n    if quat[3] > 1.0:\\n        quat[3] = 1.0\\n    elif quat[3] < -1.0:\\n        quat[3] = -1.0\\n\\n    den = np.sqrt(1.0 - quat[3] * quat[3])\\n    if math.isclose(den, 0.0):\\n        # This is (close to) a zero degree rotation, immediately return\\n        return np.zeros(3)\\n\\n    return (quat[:3] * 2.0 * math.acos(quat[3])) / den\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.62}}"}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_011PRTXXw3mhUPcMEww9Y3ns","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"robosuite 1.4.1 just requires `mujoco` without a version pin. So any mujoco version should work in theory. But in practice, mujoco 3.8.1 might have behavioral changes.\n\nLet me look at this from another angle. The OFT paper was submitted to arXiv in February 2025 (arXiv:2502.19645). The code was likely developed in late 2024/early 2025. At that time:\n- mujoco was at version ~3.1.x to ~3.2.x\n- robosuite 1.4.1 was released\n\n\n- MuJoCo 3.1 came out in early 2024 and 3.2 in mid-2024, so the code was probably written with one of those versions in mind.\n\nEven across minor versions, the physics engine behavior should remain consistent for the same XML models, so the differences should be minimal. But with 0% success rate across all tasks using a model that generates structured actions, I'm wondering if the real issue is how the environment is interpreting those actions. Let me examine the action magnitudes more carefully.\n\nThe OFT model outputs actions in the [-1, 1] range before unnormalization. After applying the q99 bounds for unnormalization, the values fall within reasonable ranges like 0.193 and 0.328. But in LIBERO's OSC controller, actions are scaled by the controller's max limits—translation gets scaled by max_translation (around 0.05 m per step) and rotation by max_rotation (around 0.1 rad per step), so an action of 0.2 or 0.3 would translate to very small movements.\n\nThe OFT model uses bounds_q99 normalization, where the output from the model head in [-1, 1] gets unnormalized using a formula that maps it to the range [q01, q99]. Looking at the actual output values like 0.193, these should fall within the unnormalized bounds I calculated earlier.\n\nNow I'm wondering if the issue is with how LIBERO's environment interprets these actions. The 7D action space expects [dx, dy, dz, dax, day, daz, gripper] with an OSC_POSE controller, so I should verify the controller configuration is set up correctly. I'm also considering whether the MuJoCo 3.8.1 rendering might be producing different visual features than what the model was trained on, though the structured, task-directed nature of the actions suggests the visual input is at least somewhat reasonable. Let me explore what else could be causing the mismatch.\n\nThe `done` signal might be the culprit—if LIBERO's task success detection has changed between versions, the robot could be performing the right actions but the environment isn't registering completion. I should check how LIBERO determines task success. Alternatively, I could test with a pretrained model directly from HuggingFace to isolate whether the issue is environmental or with the local checkpoint, though that would be time-consuming on AutoDL. Let me instead trace through what the robot actually does during an episode—with ~280 steps per episode and the model generating 8 actions at a time, I can see what's happening step by step.\n\nActually, I need to verify the action processing pipeline carefully. The eval script calls `process_action` which normalizes and inverts the gripper, then converts to a list for the environment.
